# Voters map, part 1: the map down to the house — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** An organiser goes from the campaign's whole area to one house with the wheel, a pinch or a click; every house in the area is on the map, coloured by the doors knocked and the support found; and the field app finds the house an agent is at anywhere in the area, not just in Kileleshwa.

**Architecture:** One PMTiles file of house outlines per map area (zoom 16 vector tiles, built once from Google Open Buildings by a script), served with the app from `public/tiles/` and read a tile at a time: by MapLibre through a custom protocol, and by the field app to match a GPS fix to a house. A new `house_visits` table, written by `record_door`, records every door knocked at a house; two SQL functions sum it per ward and per house for the colours. The Voters screen becomes a full-screen map whose level follows the zoom, with a panel (a bottom sheet on phones) and a People tab.

**Tech Stack:** TanStack Start/Router, React 19, React Query 5, MapLibre GL 6.11, Supabase (Postgres, RLS), plain `tsx` test scripts and SQL tests. No new npm packages (see Decisions).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-voters-map-design.md` (sections 1, 2 "Finding the house", 4 "Houses", "The campaign's map area" and "Door visits and houses", 6, 7, 8 and part 1 of section 9). Read it before starting.

## Decisions this plan makes

The spec leaves these open, or they had to change once the code was read. Each is small and reversible.

1. **The map area lives on `campaigns.map_area`, not `atlas_settings`.** `atlas_settings` has no row until the candidate or manager picks a home area, its `home_area` is required and points at atlas areas Lovable hasn't loaded yet (the figures migration `20261007100000_atlas_first_counties` is still pending there), and only Groundwork can set a map area, because it needs a house file.
2. **No new npm packages.** The spec names `@mapbox/vector-tile` and `pbf` for the phone's lookup, and part 2 will want `d3-delaunay` and `polygon-clipping`. `bun.lock` is written by Lovable's bot and bun isn't installed here, so a dependency added from this side risks breaking Lovable's build. Reading and writing PMTiles and vector tiles for one layer of polygons is about 400 lines of tested code (Tasks 1 and 2). MapLibre's own reader (already in `node_modules`) checks the tiles in a test.
3. **Constituency outlines wait for part 2.** The ward shapes were simplified one by one, so neighbouring wards' borders don't share points (Embakasi North cancels 5 of its 80 edges), and tracing a constituency's outline from them leaves slivers. At the area level the constituencies are named on the map instead; a click on a name flies to the constituency.
4. **Colour by:** Doors and Support (both over the last 30 days: section 6 has the map colour "the houses visited in 30 days"), plus **2022 vote**, which keeps today's atlas shading (each ward shaded by its constituency's 2022 result for the campaign's race, ahead or behind) until part 4 brings results by polling station. The old "Progress" shading and the ward list "largest gaps first" give way to "Doors first".
5. **A click goes one level down:** on a ward at the area or constituency level, into the ward; on a constituency's name, into the constituency; anywhere inside a ward at the ward level, to street level there; on a house, its card. **The area follows the camera:** when the person moves the map (not when the app flies it), the area becomes the ward or constituency under the map's centre, for the zoom on show.
6. **`house_visits.ward_id` is the person's ward.** Every part 1 visit has a person, and the field app's walk list is the ward the agent chose; part 3 adds the ward for visits with no person.
7. **Agents read the house visits they made** (part 3 adds the houses on their lists). The office (super, candidate, manager, organiser) reads every visit in the campaign.
8. **Door events copied in keep no score, except one:** a person's latest conversation at the door takes their current score, the "last score" the spec says a house takes. Older visits keep none.
9. **Kalonzo's public site keeps its ward question off.** With Nairobi's 85 wards in his workspace, the volunteer form would otherwise ask supporters anywhere in Kenya to pick a Nairobi ward. A presidential campaign's site never lists wards.
10. **The People list's houses come from `people.building_id`**, which the field app already sets; a person found by search flies to their house by decoding its plus code, which needs no new column.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include these. Values are the spec's.

- Levels by zoom: area 8 to 11, constituency 11 to 13, ward 13 to 16, street 16 to 20. Mathira's area is one constituency, so it starts at the constituency level. Map zoom 8 to 20; a house opens at zoom 18. The map's bounds are the area's, with a margin.
- Layout: the map fills the screen below the app's header and the page does not scroll; a panel on the right, 340 to 440 pixels, scrolls on its own; below 1,100 pixels wide the panel is a sheet over the bottom of the map that drags up to half or full height. Crumbs and one search box sit above the map.
- A line on the map says what a click or a scroll does at the level on show (AGENTS.md: "ward/building actions stay explained in-map").
- Houses: Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0), `183_buildings.csv.gz`, confidence 0.65 or more, the centre inside one of the area's wards. Zoom 16 tiles only, one layer `buildings`, one property `pc` (the plus code, the house's id), 1,024 units across, gzip. Files `public/tiles/houses-nairobi.pmtiles` and `public/tiles/houses-mathira.pmtiles`, each at most 25 MiB, with the houses per ward next to each (`houses-<id>.json`). `public/geo/buildings/` goes.
- Expected: Nairobi 805,234 houses over 85 wards (Ruai the most, 71,025; Laini Saba 410), about 14.9 MiB in 1,619 tiles; Mathira 141,692 over 6 wards, about 2.6 MiB in 827 tiles.
- "Which house am I at?": the outline the fix is in, else the nearest within 30 metres when the position is good to 50 metres, else a tap on one within 80 metres (`lib/geo.ts`, `useDoorPlace`), everywhere in the campaign's area.
- Map areas: `nairobi` for Sakaja and Kalonzo, `nyeri/mathira` for Mathira. Kalonzo gets Nairobi's 85 wards with registered voters from the 2022 register and no targets. A campaign with no map area sees a line saying so, not Nairobi.
- `house_visits`: the house's plus code, its ward, outcome, support, issue, agent, when, where, the person, and the phone-made id that keeps a retried sync from counting twice. `record_door` writes one for every visit with a house. Door events already on record with a house are copied in once.
- Colours: Doors is the share of houses knocked in 30 days, and a house takes its last outcome. Support is supporters among doors that gave a score, and a house takes its last score. Nothing is guessed: no data is grey, and the legend says so.
- The address keeps the area, house, colour, base map and tab; `view=people` opens the People tab; `view=doors` and `view=wards` open the map.
- People tab: today's People view for the area on show, with "Add a person", "Manage records", "Message these people" and "People list · CSV" (renamed from "Walk list · CSV"). Agents don't get the People tab.
- "Doors first": the wards with the most houses not knocked in 30 days, up to six, each with one line of why in numbers and one button.
- Credits on the map: MapTiler and OpenStreetMap (from the base style), "Houses: Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0)", "Wards: geoBoundaries (CC BY 4.0)".
- Copy: plain words, sentence case, numbers formatted `en-KE`, as the app writes. Never record or guess a person's ethnicity. Never name TransTally.
- Repository (AGENTS.md and the user's rules): never rewrite pushed history; keep `main` working; ask before pushing and before any large download; every commit ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; the repository is public, so no secrets or voter data in it; lint only the lines you change; never run prettier on `data/atlas/REPORT.md`.
- Database: this is schema **23**. The area-profiles plan (branch `area-profiles`) also claims 23. Whichever of the two reaches `main` second renames its migrations to timestamps after the other's and takes the next version.
- Code runs before its migration: Lovable publishes a push before anyone asks it to apply the migration. Every read of a new column, table or function must fail soft: the screen says the house data isn't in yet, and nothing else breaks.

## Review Focus

Inputs the spec implies but no task's own tests exercise directly. Each has a test added to the task that owns the code.

1. **A campaign with no map area, or the app running before the migration:** the Voters screen shows "No map for this campaign yet" with the People tab working, and the field app records visits by GPS. Test: `readMapArea` returns null when the column is missing (Task 6); `houseStatus` returns "none" with no area (Task 8).
2. **A phone outside the area or with no fix:** an agent in Thika, or indoors: "Which house am I at?" says the place isn't on the house map and the visit still saves. Test: `houseStatus` for a fix outside the area and for no fix (Task 8).
3. **A bad house or area in the address:** a hand-edited link with `house=hello` or `area=w:nowhere` opens the area without flying to NaN or crashing. Tests: `validateVotersSearch` drops a bad house (Task 11); `cameraFor` falls back to the whole area for an unknown ward (Task 7).
4. **Houses with no counts, or a ward with houses but no visits:** Doors shows grey "No houses on the map", not red or "NaN%"; "Doors first" skips wards it can't count. Tests: `wardShade` with zero houses, `doorsFirst` without counts (Task 6).
5. **Old links:** `/canvassing`, `/people?person=p-9`, `/voters?view=wards&area=c:Westlands` and the Overview's links land on the right tab and area. Tests: `validateVotersSearch` and `peopleForward` (Task 11).

How to run things (from the repository root; `$SCRATCH` below is the session's scratchpad directory, where downloads and throwaway files go):

- One test file: `npx -y tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json tests/<name>.test.ts`
- Everything: `npm test` (every `tests/*.test.ts`, then `tests/sql/run.sh`, which needs Postgres 16: it is installed here)
- The SQL tests alone: `bash tests/sql/run.sh`. Postgres won't start as root, so in a cloud session (root, Postgres 16 under `/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin`) run them as the postgres user: `chmod -R a+rX . && su postgres -s /bin/bash -c "cd $PWD && PATH=/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin:\$PATH bash tests/sql/run.sh"` (every folder above the checkout needs `a+x` too).
- Types: `npx tsc --noEmit`
- Lint the files you touched: `npx eslint <files>`; format them: `npx prettier --write <files>`
- Build: `npm run build`

### Task 1: PMTiles archives

**Files:**
- Create: `src/lib/varint.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/pmtiles.ts`
- Test: `tests/pmtiles.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces:
  - `writeVarint(out: number[], n: number): void`, `readVarint(b: Uint8Array, at: { pos: number }): number`
  - `type Entry = { tileId: number; offset: number; length: number; runLength: number }`
  - `type Header = { rootOffset: number; rootLength: number; tileDataOffset: number; tileCount: number; minZoom: number; maxZoom: number; bounds: [number, number, number, number] }`
  - `type TileIn = { z: number; x: number; y: number; data: Uint8Array }`
  - `type ArchiveInfo = { minZoom: number; maxZoom: number; bounds: [number, number, number, number]; center: [number, number, number]; metadata: Record<string, unknown> }`
  - `type Archive = { header: Header; getTile(z: number, x: number, y: number): Promise<Uint8Array | null> }`
  - `type ReadRange = (offset: number, length: number) => Promise<Uint8Array>`
  - `zxyToTileId(z, x, y): number`, `serializeDirectory(entries): Uint8Array`, `parseDirectory(b): Entry[]`, `findEntry(entries, tileId): Entry | null`
  - `writeArchive(tiles: TileIn[], info: ArchiveInfo, gzip: (b: Uint8Array) => Uint8Array): Uint8Array`
  - `readHeader(b: Uint8Array): Header`
  - `openArchive(read: ReadRange, gunzip: (b: Uint8Array) => Promise<Uint8Array>): Promise<Archive>`
  - `gunzip(b: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array>`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `tests/pmtiles.test.ts`:

```ts
// Checks for the PMTiles archives the house files use: tile ids along the
// Hilbert curve, the directory, and a file written then read back a tile at a
// time. Pure; nothing leaves this process. Run from the repository root:
//   npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json tests/pmtiles.test.ts

import { gzipSync } from "node:zlib";

import {
  findEntry,
  gunzip,
  openArchive,
  parseDirectory,
  readHeader,
  serializeDirectory,
  writeArchive,
  zxyToTileId,
  type ArchiveInfo,
  type TileIn,
} from "@/lib/pmtiles";

let pass = 0;
let fail = 0;

function eq(name: string, got: unknown, want: unknown) {
  const g = JSON.stringify(got);
  const w = JSON.stringify(want);
  if (g === w) pass++;
  else {
    fail++;
    console.log(`FAIL ${name}\n  got  ${g}\n  want ${w}`);
  }
}

function throws(name: string, f: () => unknown, message: RegExp) {
  try {
    f();
    fail++;
    console.log(`FAIL ${name}: nothing was thrown`);
  } catch (e) {
    const m = e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e);
    if (message.test(m)) pass++;
    else {
      fail++;
      console.log(`FAIL ${name}: threw "${m}"`);
    }
  }
}

const gz = (b: Uint8Array) => new Uint8Array(gzipSync(b));

eq(
  "tile ids from the PMTiles spec",
  [
    zxyToTileId(0, 0, 0),
    zxyToTileId(1, 0, 0),
    zxyToTileId(1, 0, 1),
    zxyToTileId(1, 1, 1),
    zxyToTileId(1, 1, 0),
    zxyToTileId(2, 0, 0),
    zxyToTileId(12, 3423, 1763),
  ],
  [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 19078479],
);
{
  const ids = new Set<number>();
  for (let x = 0; x < 8; x++) for (let y = 0; y < 8; y++) ids.add(zxyToTileId(3, x, y));
  eq("zoom 3's 64 tiles each have an id, after zoom 2's", [ids.size, Math.min(...ids), Math.max(...ids)], [64, 21, 84]);
}
throws("no such tile", () => zxyToTileId(1, 2, 0), /no tile 1\/2\/0/);

const entries = [
  { tileId: 5, offset: 0, length: 10, runLength: 1 },
  { tileId: 7, offset: 10, length: 4, runLength: 1 },
  { tileId: 300000000, offset: 99, length: 1, runLength: 1 },
];
eq("a directory reads back as written", parseDirectory(serializeDirectory(entries)), entries);
eq("a tile is found by its id", findEntry(entries, 7), entries[1]);
eq(
  "and a tile not there isn't",
  [findEntry(entries, 6), findEntry(entries, 4), findEntry(entries, 400000000)],
  [null, null, null],
);

const INFO: ArchiveInfo = {
  minZoom: 16,
  maxZoom: 16,
  bounds: [36.66, -1.45, 37.11, -1.16],
  center: [36.82, -1.29, 16],
  metadata: { name: "test" },
};
const text = (s: string) => new TextEncoder().encode(s);
const TILES: TileIn[] = [
  { z: 16, x: 39460, y: 32998, data: text("first") },
  { z: 16, x: 39461, y: 32998, data: text("second") },
  { z: 16, x: 39460, y: 32999, data: text("third") },
];
const file = writeArchive(TILES, INFO, gz);
const archive = await openArchive(async (offset, length) => file.subarray(offset, offset + length), gunzip);
const read = async (z: number, x: number, y: number) => {
  const b = await archive.getTile(z, x, y);
  return b ? new TextDecoder().decode(b) : null;
};
eq(
  "each tile reads back",
  [await read(16, 39460, 32998), await read(16, 39461, 32998), await read(16, 39460, 32999)],
  ["first", "second", "third"],
);
eq("a tile the file doesn't have", await read(16, 39462, 32998), null);
eq("a zoom the file doesn't have", await read(15, 19730, 16499), null);
eq(
  "the header says what the file holds",
  (({ minZoom, maxZoom, tileCount, bounds }) => ({ minZoom, maxZoom, tileCount, bounds }))(
    readHeader(file),
  ),
  { minZoom: 16, maxZoom: 16, tileCount: 3, bounds: [36.66, -1.45, 37.11, -1.16] },
);
throws("not a PMTiles file", () => readHeader(new Uint8Array(127)), /Not a PMTiles v3 file/);
throws("two tiles at one place", () => writeArchive([TILES[0]!, TILES[0]!], INFO, gz), /Two tiles/);
{
  // 6,000 tiles scattered across the world: a directory too big for the first read.
  const many: TileIn[] = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < 6000; i++)
    many.push({ z: 16, x: (i * 7919) % 65536, y: (i * 104729) % 65536, data: Uint8Array.of(i % 251) });
  throws("too many tiles for one directory", () => writeArchive(many, INFO, gz), /Too many tiles/);
}

console.log(`${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `src/lib/varint.ts`**

```ts
// Unsigned varints (LEB128), as protobuf and PMTiles store whole numbers: seven
// bits a byte, low bits first. Exact up to 2^53, as JavaScript numbers are.

/** Appends n to out. */
export function writeVarint(out: number[], n: number): void {
  let v = n;
  while (v >= 128) {
    out.push((v % 128) | 128);
    v = Math.floor(v / 128);
  }
  out.push(v);
}

/** The varint at at.pos, moving at.pos past it. */
export function readVarint(b: Uint8Array, at: { pos: number }): number {
  let n = 0;
  let scale = 1;
  for (;;) {
    const byte = b[at.pos++];
    if (byte === undefined) throw new Error("A number runs past the end of the data.");
    n += (byte & 127) * scale;
    if (byte < 128) return n;
    scale *= 128;
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `src/lib/pmtiles.ts`**

```ts
// PMTiles v3 (https://github.com/protomaps/PMTiles/blob/main/spec/v3/spec.md):
// a whole map's tiles in one file, read a tile at a time with HTTP range
// requests, so a static host can serve it. This writes the house files
// (scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts) and reads them in the browser. It holds only
// what they use: one root directory and no leaf directories, gzip for the
// directory, the metadata and the tiles, and vector tiles. Pure apart from the
// gzip and the reading handed in, so it runs the same in tests and browsers.

import { readVarint, writeVarint } from "@/lib/varint";

/** Where a tile's bytes are in the tile data. A run length of 0 marks a leaf directory. */
export type Entry = { tileId: number; offset: number; length: number; runLength: number };

export type Header = {
  rootOffset: number;
  rootLength: number;
  tileDataOffset: number;
  tileCount: number;
  minZoom: number;
  maxZoom: number;
  /** West, south, east, north, in degrees. */
  bounds: [number, number, number, number];
};

/** A tile to write, not yet compressed. */
export type TileIn = { z: number; x: number; y: number; data: Uint8Array };

export type ArchiveInfo = {
  minZoom: number;
  maxZoom: number;
  bounds: [number, number, number, number];
  /** Longitude, latitude and zoom to open at. */
  center: [number, number, number];
  metadata: Record<string, unknown>;
};

export type Archive = {
  header: Header;
  /** The tile's bytes, uncompressed; null when the file has no such tile. */
  getTile(z: number, x: number, y: number): Promise<Uint8Array | null>;
};

/** Reads length bytes from offset; fewer when the file ends first. */
export type ReadRange = (offset: number, length: number) => Promise<Uint8Array>;

const HEADER_BYTES = 127;
/** The header and root directory are in the first 16 KiB, so one request reads both. */
const FIRST_READ = 16_384;
const GZIP = 2;
const MVT = 1;
const U32 = 2 ** 32;

/** A tile's place along the Hilbert curve, after every tile of the zooms below it. */
export function zxyToTileId(z: number, x: number, y: number): number {
  if (z > 26) throw new Error("Zoom past 26 is beyond whole-number precision.");
  const n = 2 ** z;
  if (!Number.isInteger(x) || !Number.isInteger(y) || x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= n || y >= n)
    throw new Error(`There is no tile ${z}/${x}/${y}.`);
  let tx = x;
  let ty = y;
  let d = 0;
  for (let s = n / 2; s >= 1; s /= 2) {
    const rx = (tx & s) > 0 ? 1 : 0;
    const ry = (ty & s) > 0 ? 1 : 0;
    d += s * s * ((3 * rx) ^ ry);
    if (ry === 0) {
      if (rx === 1) {
        tx = n - 1 - tx;
        ty = n - 1 - ty;
      }
      [tx, ty] = [ty, tx];
    }
  }
  return (4 ** z - 1) / 3 + d;
}

export function serializeDirectory(entries: Entry[]): Uint8Array {
  const out: number[] = [];
  writeVarint(out, entries.length);
  let last = 0;
  for (const e of entries) {
    writeVarint(out, e.tileId - last);
    last = e.tileId;
  }
  for (const e of entries) writeVarint(out, e.runLength);
  for (const e of entries) writeVarint(out, e.length);
  entries.forEach((e, i) => {
    const prev = entries[i - 1];
    // 0 means "straight after the previous tile", which most are.
    writeVarint(out, prev && e.offset === prev.offset + prev.length ? 0 : e.offset + 1);
  });
  return Uint8Array.from(out);
}

export function parseDirectory(b: Uint8Array): Entry[] {
  const at = { pos: 0 };
  const count = readVarint(b, at);
  const entries: Entry[] = [];
  let id = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    id += readVarint(b, at);
    entries.push({ tileId: id, offset: 0, length: 0, runLength: 0 });
  }
  for (const e of entries) e.runLength = readVarint(b, at);
  for (const e of entries) e.length = readVarint(b, at);
  entries.forEach((e, i) => {
    const v = readVarint(b, at);
    const prev = entries[i - 1];
    e.offset = v === 0 && prev ? prev.offset + prev.length : v - 1;
  });
  return entries;
}

/** The entry for a tile, by binary search, as the spec has it; null when there is none. */
export function findEntry(entries: Entry[], tileId: number): Entry | null {
  let lo = 0;
  let hi = entries.length - 1;
  while (lo <= hi) {
    const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1;
    const e = entries[mid]!;
    if (tileId > e.tileId) lo = mid + 1;
    else if (tileId < e.tileId) hi = mid - 1;
    else return e;
  }
  // A leaf directory covers the ids after its own; a run of one tile repeated covers its run.
  const before = entries[hi];
  if (before && (before.runLength === 0 || tileId - before.tileId < before.runLength)) return before;
  return null;
}

function setU64(v: DataView, at: number, n: number): void {
  v.setUint32(at, n % U32, true);
  v.setUint32(at + 4, Math.floor(n / U32), true);
}

function getU64(v: DataView, at: number): number {
  return v.getUint32(at, true) + v.getUint32(at + 4, true) * U32;
}

/** One file holding every tile, in tile-id order. Throws if the directory won't fit the first read. */
export function writeArchive(
  tiles: TileIn[],
  info: ArchiveInfo,
  gzip: (b: Uint8Array) => Uint8Array,
): Uint8Array {
  const sorted = tiles
    .map((t) => ({ id: zxyToTileId(t.z, t.x, t.y), data: gzip(t.data) }))
    .sort((a, b) => a.id - b.id);
  const entries: Entry[] = [];
  let tileBytes = 0;
  for (const t of sorted) {
    if (entries[entries.length - 1]?.tileId === t.id) throw new Error("Two tiles at one place.");
    entries.push({ tileId: t.id, offset: tileBytes, length: t.data.length, runLength: 1 });
    tileBytes += t.data.length;
  }
  const root = gzip(serializeDirectory(entries));
  if (HEADER_BYTES + root.length > FIRST_READ)
    throw new Error(`Too many tiles for one directory (${entries.length}): split the area.`);
  const metadata = gzip(new TextEncoder().encode(JSON.stringify(info.metadata)));
  const rootOffset = HEADER_BYTES;
  const metadataOffset = rootOffset + root.length;
  const tileDataOffset = metadataOffset + metadata.length;

  const out = new Uint8Array(tileDataOffset + tileBytes);
  const v = new DataView(out.buffer);
  out.set(new TextEncoder().encode("PMTiles"), 0);
  out[7] = 3;
  setU64(v, 8, rootOffset);
  setU64(v, 16, root.length);
  setU64(v, 24, metadataOffset);
  setU64(v, 32, metadata.length);
  setU64(v, 40, tileDataOffset); // no leaf directories: an empty section
  setU64(v, 48, 0);
  setU64(v, 56, tileDataOffset);
  setU64(v, 64, tileBytes);
  setU64(v, 72, entries.length); // tiles addressed
  setU64(v, 80, entries.length); // directory entries
  setU64(v, 88, entries.length); // distinct tiles
  out[96] = 1; // clustered: tiles in id order
  out[97] = GZIP; // directory and metadata
  out[98] = GZIP; // tiles
  out[99] = MVT;
  out[100] = info.minZoom;
  out[101] = info.maxZoom;
  const e7 = (deg: number) => Math.round(deg * 1e7);
  const [west, south, east, north] = info.bounds;
  v.setInt32(102, e7(west), true);
  v.setInt32(106, e7(south), true);
  v.setInt32(110, e7(east), true);
  v.setInt32(114, e7(north), true);
  const [lng, lat, zoom] = info.center;
  out[118] = zoom;
  v.setInt32(119, e7(lng), true);
  v.setInt32(123, e7(lat), true);
  out.set(root, rootOffset);
  out.set(metadata, metadataOffset);
  let at = tileDataOffset;
  for (const t of sorted) {
    out.set(t.data, at);
    at += t.data.length;
  }
  return out;
}

export function readHeader(b: Uint8Array): Header {
  if (
    b.length < HEADER_BYTES ||
    new TextDecoder().decode(b.subarray(0, 7)) !== "PMTiles" ||
    b[7] !== 3
  )
    throw new Error("Not a PMTiles v3 file.");
  if (b[97] !== GZIP || b[98] !== GZIP || b[99] !== MVT)
    throw new Error("Only gzipped vector tiles are read here.");
  const v = new DataView(b.buffer, b.byteOffset, b.byteLength);
  if (getU64(v, 48) > 0) throw new Error("Leaf directories aren't read here.");
  const deg = (at: number) => v.getInt32(at, true) / 1e7;
  return {
    rootOffset: getU64(v, 8),
    rootLength: getU64(v, 16),
    tileDataOffset: getU64(v, 56),
    tileCount: getU64(v, 72),
    minZoom: b[100]!,
    maxZoom: b[101]!,
    bounds: [deg(102), deg(106), deg(110), deg(114)],
  };
}

/** An archive read through `read`: the header and directory once, then a tile per call. */
export async function openArchive(
  read: ReadRange,
  unzip: (b: Uint8Array) => Promise<Uint8Array>,
): Promise<Archive> {
  const first = await read(0, FIRST_READ);
  const header = readHeader(first);
  const rootEnd = header.rootOffset + header.rootLength;
  if (rootEnd > first.length) throw new Error("The directory isn't where the header says.");
  const entries = parseDirectory(await unzip(first.subarray(header.rootOffset, rootEnd)));
  return {
    header,
    async getTile(z, x, y) {
      if (z < header.minZoom || z > header.maxZoom) return null;
      const n = 2 ** z;
      if (!Number.isInteger(x) || !Number.isInteger(y) || x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= n || y >= n)
        return null;
      const e = findEntry(entries, zxyToTileId(z, x, y));
      if (!e) return null;
      if (e.runLength === 0) throw new Error("Leaf directories aren't read here.");
      return unzip(await read(header.tileDataOffset + e.offset, e.length));
    },
  };
}

/** gzip undone with the platform's own streams, in browsers and Node alike. */
export async function gunzip(b: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array> {
  const stream = new Blob([new Uint8Array(b)]).stream().pipeThrough(new DecompressionStream("gzip"));
  return new Uint8Array(await new Response(stream).arrayBuffer());
}
```

After `ringCentre`, add:

```ts
/** The area inside a closed ring, in square metres (flat: well under 1% off for a house). */
export function ringAreaM2(ring: Ring): number {
  const origin = ring[0];
  if (!origin) return 0;
  let twice = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < ring.length - 1; i++) {
    const [x1, y1] = toLocalM(ring[i]!, origin);
    const [x2, y2] = toLocalM(ring[i + 1]!, origin);
    twice += x1 * y2 - x2 * y1;
  }
  return Math.abs(twice) / 2;
}
```

At the end of the file, add:

```ts
/** A ward's outline and slug, for finding the ward a point is in. */
export type WardShape = { slug: string; polygons: Ring[][]; box: Box };

type ShapeFile = {
  features: { properties: { slug: string }; geometry: { type: string; coordinates: unknown } }[];
};

/** The wards of a ward file (public/geo/*-wards.json), Polygon or MultiPolygon. */
export function wardShapes(geo: ShapeFile): WardShape[] {
  return geo.features.map((f) => ({
    slug: f.properties.slug,
    polygons: (f.geometry.type === "MultiPolygon"
      ? f.geometry.coordinates
      : [f.geometry.coordinates]) as Ring[][],
    box: boundsOf(f.geometry),
  }));
}

/** The ward a point is in, if any. */
export function wardAt(p: LngLat, shapes: WardShape[]): WardShape | null {
  return (
    shapes.find((w) => inBox(p, w.box) && w.polygons.some((rings) => inPolygon(p, rings))) ?? null
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `src/lib/pluscode.ts`**

```ts
// Plus codes (Open Location Code, https://github.com/google/open-location-code):
// each house in the house files is named by the plus code of its centre, which
// also says where it is, to about 3 metres. Pure.

import type { LngLat } from "@/lib/geo";

const DIGITS = "23456789CFGHJMPQRVWX";

/**
 * A full plus code as Open Buildings gives each house: 8 digits, "+", then 2 to 7;
 * the first two (latitude, then longitude) only as far as the world goes.
 */
export const PLUS_CODE =
  /^[23456789C][23456789CFGHJMPQRV][23456789CFGHJMPQRVWX]{6}\+[23456789CFGHJMPQRVWX]{2,7}$/;

/** The centre of the area a full plus code names; null if it isn't one. */
export function plusCodeCentre(code: string): LngLat | null {
  const c = code.trim().toUpperCase();
  if (!PLUS_CODE.test(c)) return null;
  const d = c.replace("+", "");
  // Five pairs of latitude and longitude digits, each 20 times finer than the last.
  let lat = -90;
  let lng = -180;
  let res = 20;
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i += 2) {
    lat += DIGITS.indexOf(d[i]!) * res;
    lng += DIGITS.indexOf(d[i + 1]!) * res;
    if (i < 8) res /= 20;
  }
  // Then a 5-row, 4-column grid per digit.
  let latRes = res;
  let lngRes = res;
  for (const ch of d.slice(10)) {
    latRes /= 5;
    lngRes /= 4;
    const v = DIGITS.indexOf(ch);
    lat += Math.floor(v / 4) * latRes;
    lng += (v % 4) * lngRes;
  }
  return [lng + lngRes / 2, lat + latRes / 2];
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `src/lib/house-tiles.ts`**

```ts
// The house layer of the map's tiles: each house's outline and plus code, at
// zoom 16 only, as Mapbox Vector Tiles
// (https://github.com/mapbox/vector-tile-spec/tree/master/2.1), 1,024 units
// across. The house script writes them; MapLibre draws them, stretching the
// zoom-16 tiles up to 20; and the field app reads the tiles around a phone to
// find the house it is at. A house that crosses a tile edge is written whole
// into each tile it touches, and each tile draws only its own part. Pure.

import { ringAreaM2, ringCentre, type Building, type LngLat, type Ring } from "@/lib/geo";
import { readVarint, writeVarint } from "@/lib/varint";

export const HOUSE_ZOOM = 16;
export const EXTENT = 1024;
export const LAYER = "buildings";

const WORLD = 2 ** HOUSE_ZOOM;
const RAD = Math.PI / 180;
const POLYGON = 3;
const MOVE_TO = 1;
const LINE_TO = 2;
const CLOSE_PATH = 7;
const UTF8_OUT = new TextEncoder();
const UTF8_IN = new TextDecoder();

/** A point on the zoom-16 grid, in tiles: 0 to 65,536 across, y down. */
export type TilePoint = [number, number];

export function toTileSpace([lng, lat]: LngLat): TilePoint {
  const s = Math.sin(lat * RAD);
  return [((lng + 180) / 360) * WORLD, (0.5 - Math.log((1 + s) / (1 - s)) / (4 * Math.PI)) * WORLD];
}

export function fromTileSpace([x, y]: TilePoint): LngLat {
  const n = Math.PI - (2 * Math.PI * y) / WORLD;
  return [(x / WORLD) * 360 - 180, Math.atan(Math.sinh(n)) / RAD];
}

/** A house as the tiles keep it: whole grid units (EXTENT to a tile), rings not closed. */
export type TileHouse = { pc: string; rings: [number, number][][] };

/** Twice the signed area: positive when the ring turns clockwise on screen (y down). */
function area2(r: [number, number][]): number {
  let a = 0;
  for (let i = 0, j = r.length - 1; i < r.length; j = i++)
    a += r[j]![0] * r[i]![1] - r[i]![0] * r[j]![1];
  return a;
}

/** A house's GeoJSON rings on the tile grid; null when nothing of its outline is left at this scale. */
export function toTileHouse(pc: string, rings: Ring[]): TileHouse | null {
  const out: [number, number][][] = [];
  for (const [i, ring] of rings.entries()) {
    const pts: [number, number][] = [];
    for (const p of ring) {
      const [x, y] = toTileSpace(p);
      const q: [number, number] = [Math.round(x * EXTENT), Math.round(y * EXTENT)];
      const last = pts[pts.length - 1];
      if (!last || last[0] !== q[0] || last[1] !== q[1]) pts.push(q);
    }
    // GeoJSON repeats the first point at the end; the tile format closes rings itself.
    const first = pts[0];
    const end = pts[pts.length - 1];
    if (pts.length > 1 && first && end && first[0] === end[0] && first[1] === end[1]) pts.pop();
    if (pts.length >= 3 && area2(pts) !== 0) out.push(pts);
    else if (i === 0) return null;
  }
  return out.length ? { pc, rings: out } : null;
}

/** The zoom-16 tiles a house touches. */
export function tilesOf(h: TileHouse): { x: number; y: number }[] {
  let minX = Infinity;
  let minY = Infinity;
  let maxX = -Infinity;
  let maxY = -Infinity;
  for (const [x, y] of h.rings[0] ?? []) {
    minX = Math.min(minX, x);
    minY = Math.min(minY, y);
    maxX = Math.max(maxX, x);
    maxY = Math.max(maxY, y);
  }
  const out: { x: number; y: number }[] = [];
  for (let x = Math.floor(minX / EXTENT); x <= Math.floor(maxX / EXTENT); x++)
    for (let y = Math.floor(minY / EXTENT); y <= Math.floor(maxY / EXTENT); y++) out.push({ x, y });
  return out;
}

/** Just enough protobuf to write a vector tile. */
class Writer {
  private bytes: number[] = [];

  varint(field: number, n: number): this {
    writeVarint(this.bytes, field * 8);
    writeVarint(this.bytes, n);
    return this;
  }

  message(field: number, body: Uint8Array): this {
    writeVarint(this.bytes, field * 8 + 2);
    writeVarint(this.bytes, body.length);
    for (const b of body) this.bytes.push(b);
    return this;
  }

  text(field: number, s: string): this {
    return this.message(field, UTF8_OUT.encode(s));
  }

  packed(field: number, ns: number[]): this {
    const body: number[] = [];
    for (const n of ns) writeVarint(body, n);
    return this.message(field, Uint8Array.from(body));
  }

  finish(): Uint8Array {
    return Uint8Array.from(this.bytes);
  }
}

const command = (id: number, count: number) => (id & 7) | (count << 3);
const zigzag = (n: number) => (n << 1) ^ (n >> 31);
const unzigzag = (n: number) => (n >>> 1) ^ -(n & 1);

/** Drawing commands for a house in the tile whose corner is (ox, oy) on the grid. */
function geometry(h: TileHouse, ox: number, oy: number): number[] {
  const out: number[] = [];
  let cx = 0;
  let cy = 0;
  h.rings.forEach((ring, i) => {
    // The outline turns clockwise on screen, its holes the other way.
    const pts = area2(ring) > 0 === (i === 0) ? ring : [...ring].reverse();
    pts.forEach(([gx, gy], k) => {
      if (k === 0) out.push(command(MOVE_TO, 1));
      if (k === 1) out.push(command(LINE_TO, pts.length - 1));
      out.push(zigzag(gx - ox - cx), zigzag(gy - oy - cy));
      cx = gx - ox;
      cy = gy - oy;
    });
    out.push(command(CLOSE_PATH, 1));
  });
  return out;
}

/** One tile of houses, as a vector tile with one layer, "buildings". */
export function encodeTile(x: number, y: number, houses: TileHouse[]): Uint8Array {
  const layer = new Writer().varint(15, 2).text(1, LAYER);
  houses.forEach((h, i) => {
    layer.message(
      2,
      new Writer()
        .packed(2, [0, i])
        .varint(3, POLYGON)
        .packed(4, geometry(h, x * EXTENT, y * EXTENT))
        .finish(),
    );
  });
  layer.text(3, "pc");
  for (const h of houses) layer.message(4, new Writer().text(1, h.pc).finish());
  layer.varint(5, EXTENT);
  return new Writer().message(3, layer.finish()).finish();
}

/** Just enough protobuf to read one back. */
class Reader {
  private readonly b: Uint8Array;
  private readonly at = { pos: 0 };

  constructor(b: Uint8Array) {
    this.b = b;
  }

  get more(): boolean {
    return this.at.pos < this.b.length;
  }

  varint(): number {
    return readVarint(this.b, this.at);
  }

  key(): { field: number; wire: number } {
    const k = this.varint();
    return { field: Math.floor(k / 8), wire: k % 8 };
  }

  bytes(): Uint8Array {
    const n = this.varint();
    const s = this.b.subarray(this.at.pos, this.at.pos + n);
    this.at.pos += n;
    return s;
  }

  skip(wire: number): void {
    if (wire === 0) this.varint();
    else if (wire === 2) {
      const n = this.varint();
      this.at.pos += n;
    } else if (wire === 1) this.at.pos += 8;
    else if (wire === 5) this.at.pos += 4;
    else throw new Error(`Not a vector tile (wire type ${wire}).`);
  }
}

function packed(b: Uint8Array): number[] {
  const r = new Reader(b);
  const out: number[] = [];
  while (r.more) out.push(r.varint());
  return out;
}

type RawFeature = { tags: number[]; type: number; geometry: number[] };
type RawLayer = {
  name: string;
  extent: number;
  keys: string[];
  values: (string | null)[];
  features: RawFeature[];
};

function readFeature(b: Uint8Array): RawFeature {
  const r = new Reader(b);
  const f: RawFeature = { tags: [], type: 0, geometry: [] };
  while (r.more) {
    const { field, wire } = r.key();
    if (field === 2 && wire === 2) f.tags = packed(r.bytes());
    else if (field === 3 && wire === 0) f.type = r.varint();
    else if (field === 4 && wire === 2) f.geometry = packed(r.bytes());
    else r.skip(wire);
  }
  return f;
}

/** A Value's string; null for numbers and the rest. */
function readText(b: Uint8Array): string | null {
  const r = new Reader(b);
  let s: string | null = null;
  while (r.more) {
    const { field, wire } = r.key();
    if (field === 1 && wire === 2) s = UTF8_IN.decode(r.bytes());
    else r.skip(wire);
  }
  return s;
}

function readLayer(b: Uint8Array): RawLayer {
  const r = new Reader(b);
  const layer: RawLayer = { name: "", extent: 4096, keys: [], values: [], features: [] };
  while (r.more) {
    const { field, wire } = r.key();
    if (field === 1 && wire === 2) layer.name = UTF8_IN.decode(r.bytes());
    else if (field === 2 && wire === 2) layer.features.push(readFeature(r.bytes()));
    else if (field === 3 && wire === 2) layer.keys.push(UTF8_IN.decode(r.bytes()));
    else if (field === 4 && wire === 2) layer.values.push(readText(r.bytes()));
    else if (field === 5 && wire === 0) layer.extent = r.varint();
    else r.skip(wire);
  }
  return layer;
}

/** Drawing commands back to rings, in the tile's own units. */
function ringsOf(g: number[]): [number, number][][] {
  const rings: [number, number][][] = [];
  let ring: [number, number][] = [];
  let x = 0;
  let y = 0;
  let i = 0;
  while (i < g.length) {
    const c = g[i++]!;
    const id = c & 7;
    if (id === CLOSE_PATH) {
      if (ring.length >= 3) rings.push(ring);
      ring = [];
      continue;
    }
    for (let k = 0; k < c >> 3; k++) {
      if (i + 1 >= g.length) return rings;
      x += unzigzag(g[i++]!);
      y += unzigzag(g[i++]!);
      if (id === MOVE_TO) ring = [[x, y]];
      else ring.push([x, y]);
    }
  }
  return rings;
}

const closed = (r: LngLat[]): LngLat[] => (r.length ? [...r, r[0]!] : r);

function layerHouses(layer: RawLayer, x: number, y: number): Building[] {
  const pcKey = layer.keys.indexOf("pc");
  const out: Building[] = [];
  for (const f of layer.features) {
    if (f.type !== POLYGON) continue;
    let pc: string | null = null;
    for (let i = 0; i + 1 < f.tags.length; i += 2)
      if (f.tags[i] === pcKey) pc = layer.values[f.tags[i + 1]!] ?? null;
    const local = ringsOf(f.geometry);
    const first = local[0];
    if (!pc || !first) continue;
    // The first ring's turn marks an outline; rings turning the other way are its holes.
    const outward = area2(first) > 0;
    const rings = [first];
    for (const r of local.slice(1)) {
      if (area2(r) > 0 === outward) break; // a second outline: a house here is one outline
      rings.push(r);
    }
    const geo = rings.map((r) =>
      closed(r.map(([lx, ly]) => fromTileSpace([x + lx / layer.extent, y + ly / layer.extent]))),
    );
    const outline = geo[0]!;
    const holes = geo.slice(1).reduce((s, h) => s + ringAreaM2(h), 0);
    out.push({
      id: pc,
      rings: geo,
      centre: ringCentre(outline),
      area: Math.round(ringAreaM2(outline) - holes),
    });
  }
  return out;
}

/** The houses in one zoom-16 tile, as outlines with their plus codes. */
export function decodeTile(x: number, y: number, bytes: Uint8Array): Building[] {
  const tile = new Reader(bytes);
  while (tile.more) {
    const { field, wire } = tile.key();
    if (field === 3 && wire === 2) {
      const layer = readLayer(tile.bytes());
      if (layer.name === LAYER) return layerHouses(layer, x, y);
    } else tile.skip(wire);
  }
  return [];
}

/** The zoom-16 tiles within radiusM of a point. */
export function tilesAround(p: LngLat, radiusM: number): { x: number; y: number }[] {
  const dLat = radiusM / 111_320;
  const dLng = dLat / Math.max(Math.cos(p[1] * RAD), 0.01);
  const [x0, y0] = toTileSpace([p[0] - dLng, p[1] + dLat]);
  const [x1, y1] = toTileSpace([p[0] + dLng, p[1] - dLat]);
  const out: { x: number; y: number }[] = [];
  for (let x = Math.floor(x0); x <= Math.floor(x1); x++)
    for (let y = Math.floor(y0); y <= Math.floor(y1); y++) out.push({ x, y });
  return out;
}

/** Houses from several tiles, each once: a house on a tile edge is in both. */
export function mergeHouses(lists: Building[][]): Building[] {
  const seen = new Map<string, Building>();
  for (const list of lists) for (const b of list) if (!seen.has(b.id)) seen.set(b.id, b);
  return [...seen.values()];
}
```

```ts
// Checks for building the house files: reading Open Buildings rows, keeping
// the houses inside an area's wards, and the tiles they make. Pure. Run from
// the repository root:
//   npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json tests/house-build.test.ts

import { gzipSync } from "node:zlib";

import { wardShapes } from "@/lib/geo";
import {
  collect,
  countsFile,
  emptyCollected,
  MAX_FILE_BYTES,
  parseRow,
  parseWkt,
  problems,
  quickPosition,
  splitCsv,
  tilesFor,
} from "@/lib/house-build";
import { decodeTile } from "@/lib/house-tiles";
import { MAP_AREAS, mapAreaFor } from "@/lib/map-areas";
import { gunzip, openArchive, writeArchive } from "@/lib/pmtiles";

let pass = 0;
let fail = 0;

function eq(name: string, got: unknown, want: unknown) {
  const g = JSON.stringify(got);
  const w = JSON.stringify(want);
  if (g === w) pass++;
  else {
    fail++;
    console.log(`FAIL ${name}\n  got  ${g}\n  want ${w}`);
  }
}

// Two square wards side by side, each about 1.1 km across.
const WARDS = wardShapes({
  features: [
    {
      properties: { slug: "west" },
      geometry: {
        type: "Polygon",
        coordinates: [[[36.8, -1.3], [36.81, -1.3], [36.81, -1.29], [36.8, -1.29], [36.8, -1.3]]],
      },
    },
    {
      properties: { slug: "east" },
      geometry: {
        type: "MultiPolygon",
        coordinates: [[[[36.81, -1.3], [36.82, -1.3], [36.82, -1.29], [36.81, -1.29], [36.81, -1.3]]]],
      },
    },
  ],
});
const BOX = { west: 36.8, south: -1.3, east: 36.82, north: -1.29 };

/** A row as the Open Buildings CSV has it: an 11 m square around its centre. */
function row(lat: number, lng: number, confidence: number, pc: string): string {
  const d = 0.00005;
  const ring = [
    [lng - d, lat - d],
    [lng + d, lat - d],
    [lng + d, lat + d],
    [lng - d, lat + d],
    [lng - d, lat - d],
  ];
  return `${lat},${lng},121.0,${confidence},"POLYGON((${ring.map(([x, y]) => `${x} ${y}`).join(", ")}))",${pc}`;
}

eq("a quoted field keeps its commas", splitCsv('1,2,"a, b",3'), ["1", "2", "a, b", "3"]);
eq("a doubled quote is a quote", splitCsv('"say ""hi""",x'), ['say "hi"', "x"]);
eq("the position, read first", quickPosition(row(-1.295, 36.805, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+22")), [36.805, -1.295]);
eq("the header is not a position", quickPosition("latitude,longitude,area_in_meters"), null);
eq(
  "a row",
  (({ lat, lng, confidence, pc }) => ({ lat, lng, confidence, pc }))(
    parseRow(row(-1.295, 36.805, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+22"))!,
  ),
  { lat: -1.295, lng: 36.805, confidence: 0.9, pc: "6GCRPQ22+22" },
);
eq(
  "an outline with a courtyard",
  parseWkt("POLYGON ((0 0, 4 0, 4 4, 0 4, 0 0), (1 1, 2 1, 2 2, 1 1))"),
  [
    [[0, 0], [4, 0], [4, 4], [0, 4], [0, 0]],
    [[1, 1], [2, 1], [2, 2], [1, 1]],
  ],
);
eq("not a polygon", parseWkt("POINT (1 2)"), null);

const found = emptyCollected();
for (const line of [
  row(-1.295, 36.805, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+22"), // west
  row(-1.295, 36.815, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+23"), // east
  row(-1.295, 36.816, 0.5, "6GCRPQ22+24"), // east, but Google isn't sure it's a building
  row(-1.295, 36.805, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+22"), // the first one again
  row(-1.28, 36.805, 0.9, "6GCRPQ22+25"), // north of both wards
  row(-1.295, 36.806, 0.9, "not a code"), // a bad plus code
]) {
  const r = parseRow(line);
  if (r) collect(WARDS, BOX, r, found);
}
eq("houses kept, per ward", [found.houses.length, found.perWard, found.lowConfidence], [
  2,
  { west: 1, east: 1 },
  1,
]);

const tiles = tilesFor(found.houses);
const file = writeArchive(
  tiles,
  { minZoom: 16, maxZoom: 16, bounds: [36.8, -1.3, 36.82, -1.29], center: [36.81, -1.295, 16], metadata: {} },
  (b) => new Uint8Array(gzipSync(b)),
);
const archive = await openArchive(async (o, l) => file.subarray(o, o + l), gunzip);
const inFile = new Set<string>();
for (const t of tiles) {
  const b = await archive.getTile(16, t.x, t.y);
  for (const h of b ? decodeTile(t.x, t.y, b) : []) inFile.add(h.id);
}
eq("every house is in the file", [...inFile].sort(), ["6GCRPQ22+22", "6GCRPQ22+23"]);

eq("a ward with no houses stops the build", problems(WARDS, { west: 3 }, 1000), [
  "No houses in east.",
]);
eq("so does a file too big to serve", problems(WARDS, { west: 1, east: 1 }, MAX_FILE_BYTES + 1), [
  "The file is 25.0 MiB, over the 25 MiB a static file may be.",
]);
eq("the counts file", countsFile(MAP_AREAS[0]!, found, "2026-10-12"), {
  area: "nairobi",
  source: "Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0)",
  minConfidence: 0.65,
  built: "2026-10-12",
  total: 2,
  wards: { east: 1, west: 1 },
});
eq(
  "the map areas, by the key a campaign holds",
  [mapAreaFor("nairobi")?.id, mapAreaFor("nyeri/mathira")?.top, mapAreaFor("kenya"), mapAreaFor(null)],
  ["nairobi", "constituency", null, null],
);

console.log(`${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
```

```ts
// The areas the Voters map covers, by the atlas key a campaign holds in
// campaigns.map_area: the ward shapes, the house file and the count of houses
// in each ward that go with each. A new area needs its ward shapes in
// public/geo, a line here, and a run of scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts.

export type Top = "county" | "constituency";

export type MapArea = {
  /** The atlas key, as campaigns.map_area holds it. */
  key: string;
  /** In file names: public/tiles/houses-<id>.pmtiles. */
  id: string;
  name: string;
  /** A county opens on its wards by constituency; a constituency on its wards. */
  top: Top;
  wards: string;
  houses: string;
  counts: string;
  /** West, south, east, north: the wards' extent, rounded out. */
  bounds: [number, number, number, number];
};

export const MAP_AREAS: MapArea[] = [
  {
    key: "nairobi",
    id: "nairobi",
    name: "Nairobi County",
    top: "county",
    wards: "/geo/nairobi-wards.json",
    houses: "/tiles/houses-nairobi.pmtiles",
    counts: "/tiles/houses-nairobi.json",
    bounds: [36.66, -1.45, 37.11, -1.16],
  },
  {
    key: "nyeri/mathira",
    id: "mathira",
    name: "Mathira",
    top: "constituency",
    wards: "/geo/mathira-wards.json",
    houses: "/tiles/houses-mathira.pmtiles",
    counts: "/tiles/houses-mathira.json",
    bounds: [37.03, -0.57, 37.31, -0.1],
  },
];

export function mapAreaFor(key: string | null | undefined): MapArea | null {
  return MAP_AREAS.find((a) => a.key === key) ?? null;
}

/** public/tiles/houses-<id>.json: the houses in each ward, which door coverage divides by. */
export type HouseCounts = {
  area: string;
  source: string;
  minConfidence: number;
  built: string;
  total: number;
  wards: Record<string, number>;
};
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `src/lib/house-build.ts`**

```ts
// Building the house files from Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0): a row is
// kept when Google is at least 65% sure it is a building and its centre is in
// one of the area's wards, as scripts/geo/ward_buildings.py did for one ward.
// Pure; scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts reads the CSV and writes the files.

import { inBox, wardAt, type Box, type LngLat, type Ring, type WardShape } from "@/lib/geo";
import { encodeTile, HOUSE_ZOOM, tilesOf, toTileHouse, type TileHouse } from "@/lib/house-tiles";
import type { HouseCounts, MapArea } from "@/lib/map-areas";
import type { TileIn } from "@/lib/pmtiles";

export const MIN_CONFIDENCE = 0.65;
/** The largest static file the app's host (Cloudflare) serves. */
export const MAX_FILE_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024;
export const CSV_HEADER = "latitude,longitude,area_in_meters,confidence,geometry,full_plus_code";
export const SOURCE = "Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0)";
/** As record_door accepts a building id. */
const BUILDING_ID = /^[0-9A-Z+]{4,24}$/;

export type Row = { lat: number; lng: number; confidence: number; wkt: string; pc: string };

/** A CSV line's fields: a quoted field keeps its commas, and "" inside one is a quote. */
export function splitCsv(line: string): string[] {
  const out: string[] = [];
  let cur = "";
  let quoted = false;
  for (let i = 0; i < line.length; i++) {
    const ch = line[i]!;
    if (quoted) {
      if (ch !== '"') cur += ch;
      else if (line[i + 1] === '"') {
        cur += '"';
        i++;
      } else quoted = false;
    } else if (ch === '"') quoted = true;
    else if (ch === ",") {
      out.push(cur);
      cur = "";
    } else cur += ch;
  }
  out.push(cur);
  return out;
}

/** The latitude and longitude that start every row, read without splitting the rest. */
export function quickPosition(line: string): LngLat | null {
  const a = line.indexOf(",");
  const b = line.indexOf(",", a + 1);
  if (a <= 0 || b <= a + 1) return null;
  const lat = Number(line.slice(0, a));
  const lng = Number(line.slice(a + 1, b));
  return Number.isFinite(lat) && Number.isFinite(lng) ? [lng, lat] : null;
}

export function parseRow(line: string): Row | null {
  const f = splitCsv(line);
  if (f.length !== 6) return null;
  const lat = Number(f[0]);
  const lng = Number(f[1]);
  const confidence = Number(f[3]);
  if (![lat, lng, confidence].every(Number.isFinite)) return null;
  return { lat, lng, confidence, wkt: f[4]!, pc: f[5]!.trim() };
}

/** POLYGON((x y, ...), (x y, ...)): the outline, then any holes (courtyards). */
export function parseWkt(wkt: string): Ring[] | null {
  if (!/^\s*POLYGON\b/i.test(wkt)) return null;
  const rings: Ring[] = [];
  for (const m of wkt.matchAll(/\(([^()]+)\)/g)) {
    const ring: Ring = [];
    for (const pair of m[1]!.split(",")) {
      const [x, y] = pair.trim().split(/\s+/).map(Number);
      if (!Number.isFinite(x) || !Number.isFinite(y)) return null;
      ring.push([x!, y!]);
    }
    if (ring.length >= 4) rings.push(ring);
  }
  return rings.length ? rings : null;
}

export type Collected = {
  houses: TileHouse[];
  perWard: Record<string, number>;
  seen: Set<string>;
  lowConfidence: number;
};

export const emptyCollected = (): Collected => ({
  houses: [],
  perWard: {},
  seen: new Set(),
  lowConfidence: 0,
});

/** Keeps the row's house when it is in one of the wards. */
export function collect(wards: WardShape[], box: Box, row: Row, into: Collected): void {
  const p: LngLat = [row.lng, row.lat];
  if (!inBox(p, box)) return;
  const ward = wardAt(p, wards);
  if (!ward) return;
  if (row.confidence < MIN_CONFIDENCE) {
    into.lowConfidence += 1;
    return;
  }
  if (!BUILDING_ID.test(row.pc) || into.seen.has(row.pc)) return;
  const rings = parseWkt(row.wkt);
  const house = rings ? toTileHouse(row.pc, rings) : null;
  if (!house) return;
  into.seen.add(row.pc);
  into.houses.push(house);
  into.perWard[ward.slug] = (into.perWard[ward.slug] ?? 0) + 1;
}

/** Each zoom-16 tile with the houses that touch it, encoded. */
export function tilesFor(houses: TileHouse[]): TileIn[] {
  const byTile = new Map<string, { x: number; y: number; houses: TileHouse[] }>();
  for (const h of houses)
    for (const t of tilesOf(h)) {
      const k = `${t.x}/${t.y}`;
      const cur = byTile.get(k) ?? { x: t.x, y: t.y, houses: [] };
      cur.houses.push(h);
      byTile.set(k, cur);
    }
  return [...byTile.values()].map((t) => ({
    z: HOUSE_ZOOM,
    x: t.x,
    y: t.y,
    data: encodeTile(t.x, t.y, t.houses),
  }));
}

export function countsFile(area: MapArea, found: Collected, built: string): HouseCounts {
  const wards = Object.fromEntries(
    Object.entries(found.perWard).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b)),
  );
  return {
    area: area.key,
    source: SOURCE,
    minConfidence: MIN_CONFIDENCE,
    built,
    total: found.houses.length,
    wards,
  };
}

/** What stops a build: a ward with no houses, or a file the host won't serve. */
export function problems(
  wards: WardShape[],
  perWard: Record<string, number>,
  bytes: number,
): string[] {
  const out = wards.filter((w) => !perWard[w.slug]).map((w) => `No houses in ${w.slug}.`);
  if (bytes > MAX_FILE_BYTES)
    out.push(
      `The file is ${(bytes / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)} MiB, over the 25 MiB a static file may be.`,
    );
  return out;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts`**

```ts
// Builds the house files: every house in each campaign map area
// (src/lib/map-areas.ts), from Google Open Buildings v3 (CC BY 4.0), as one
// PMTiles file per area and the number of houses in each ward.
//
//   npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts <183_buildings.csv.gz>
//
// <183_buildings.csv.gz> is the S2 cell covering Nairobi and Nyeri (1.76 GB):
//   https://storage.googleapis.com/open-buildings-data/v3/polygons_s2_level_4_gzip/183_buildings.csv.gz
// Writes public/tiles/houses-<id>.pmtiles and public/tiles/houses-<id>.json,
// and nothing at all if a ward has no houses or a file is over 25 MiB.

import { createReadStream, mkdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { createInterface } from "node:readline";
import { createGunzip, gzipSync } from "node:zlib";

import { inBox, wardShapes, type Box } from "@/lib/geo";
import {
  collect,
  countsFile,
  CSV_HEADER,
  emptyCollected,
  MIN_CONFIDENCE,
  parseRow,
  problems,
  quickPosition,
  SOURCE,
  tilesFor,
} from "@/lib/house-build";
import { HOUSE_ZOOM, LAYER } from "@/lib/house-tiles";
import { MAP_AREAS } from "@/lib/map-areas";
import { writeArchive } from "@/lib/pmtiles";

const src = process.argv[2];
if (!src) {
  console.error("Usage: npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.json scripts/geo/house-tiles.ts <183_buildings.csv.gz>");
  process.exit(1);
}

const areas = MAP_AREAS.map((area) => {
  const [west, south, east, north] = area.bounds;
  const box: Box = { west, south, east, north };
  const wards = wardShapes(JSON.parse(readFileSync(`public${area.wards}`, "utf8")));
  return { area, box, wards, found: emptyCollected() };
});

const lines = createInterface({ input: createReadStream(src).pipe(createGunzip()), crlfDelay: Infinity });
let header = true;
let rows = 0;
for await (const line of lines) {
  if (header) {
    header = false;
    if (line.trim() !== CSV_HEADER) {
      console.error(`These aren't Open Buildings v3 columns: ${line}`);
      process.exit(1);
    }
    continue;
  }
  rows += 1;
  if (rows % 5_000_000 === 0) console.log(`${rows / 1_000_000} million rows read…`);
  const p = quickPosition(line);
  if (!p) continue;
  for (const a of areas) {
    if (!inBox(p, a.box)) continue;
    const row = parseRow(line);
    if (row) collect(a.wards, a.box, row, a.found);
  }
}

const built = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);
const gz = (b: Uint8Array) => new Uint8Array(gzipSync(b, { level: 9 }));
const fmt = (n: number) => n.toLocaleString("en-KE");
let stop = false;
const outputs: { path: string; file: Uint8Array; countsPath: string; counts: string }[] = [];
for (const a of areas) {
  const tiles = tilesFor(a.found.houses);
  const [w, s, e, n] = a.area.bounds;
  const file = writeArchive(
    tiles,
    {
      minZoom: HOUSE_ZOOM,
      maxZoom: HOUSE_ZOOM,
      bounds: a.area.bounds,
      center: [(w + e) / 2, (s + n) / 2, HOUSE_ZOOM],
      metadata: {
        name: `Houses: ${a.area.name}`,
        attribution: SOURCE,
        vector_layers: [{ id: LAYER, fields: { pc: "String" }, minzoom: HOUSE_ZOOM, maxzoom: HOUSE_ZOOM }],
      },
    },
    gz,
  );
  const counts = countsFile(a.area, a.found, built);
  console.log(
    `\n${a.area.name}: ${fmt(counts.total)} houses in ${fmt(tiles.length)} tiles, ` +
      `${(file.length / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)} MiB; ${fmt(a.found.lowConfidence)} below confidence ${MIN_CONFIDENCE}`,
  );
  for (const [slug, count] of Object.entries(counts.wards).sort((x, y) => y[1] - x[1]))
    console.log(`  ${slug.padEnd(32)} ${fmt(count)}`);
  for (const p of problems(a.wards, a.found.perWard, file.length)) {
    console.error(`PROBLEM ${a.area.name}: ${p}`);
    stop = true;
  }
  outputs.push({
    path: `public${a.area.houses}`,
    file,
    countsPath: `public${a.area.counts}`,
    counts: JSON.stringify(counts, null, 2) + "\n",
  });
}
if (stop) process.exit(1);
mkdirSync("public/tiles", { recursive: true });
for (const o of outputs) {
  writeFileSync(o.path, o.file);
  writeFileSync(o.countsPath, o.counts);
  console.log(`Wrote ${o.path} and ${o.countsPath}`);
}
```

### Task 5: Database

**Files:**
- Create: `supabase/migrations/20261012090000_voters_map.sql`
- Create: `tests/sql/houses.test.sql`
- Create: `src/lib/site/wards.ts`
- Create: `tests/site-wards.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/lib/site/public.server.ts` (the ward list on a published site)
- Modify: `src/lib/site.functions.ts` (the ward list on the Website screen)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `record_door` as `supabase/migrations/20260924160000_map_places.sql` defines it (the latest definition); `stamp_campaign`, `my_campaign()`, `my_campaign_role()`, `is_team_member()` from `20260928114453_*.sql`; the campaign ids `ca000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000000{1,2,3}` (Kalonzo, Sakaja, Mathira); `data/atlas/nairobi/register.csv` (2022 rows, for the numbers below).
- Produces:
  - `campaigns.map_area text` (an atlas key: `nairobi` for Sakaja and Kalonzo, `nyeri/mathira` for Mathira).
  - Kalonzo's 85 Nairobi wards (same slugs as Sakaja's).
  - `house_visits (id, campaign_id, client_id, pc, ward_id, person_id, outcome, support, issue, actor, visited_at, lat, lng, accuracy_m, created_at)`, unique `(campaign_id, client_id)`.
  - `house_doors() returns table (ward_id uuid, knocked integer, rated integer, supporters integer, doors_week integer, agents_today uuid[])` and `ward_houses(_ward_id uuid) returns table (pc text, visited_at timestamptz, outcome text, support smallint)`, both callable by `authenticated`.
  - Schema version 23.
  - `MAX_WARDS = 150` and `siteWards<T>(level: string | null | undefined, wards: T[] | null): T[]` in `src/lib/site/wards.ts`.

Why `house_doors()` counts each house once: Doors is "the share of houses knocked in 30 days" (Global Constraints), so a house knocked twice is one house. A house takes the outcome of its latest visit in 30 days and the score of its latest visit in 30 days that gave one ("a house takes its last outcome ... its last score", and the rules are today's `wardmap.ts`, which keeps a known score): a house that said 4 ten days ago and wasn't home yesterday is knocked, rated and a supporter, while a score older than 30 days drops out. Visits with no ward are left out, and ties between visits at the same moment resolve by `created_at`, then `id`.

```sql
-- The Voters map's houses: each campaign's map area, Kalonzo's wards, and the
-- doors knocked at each house (house_visits), which record_door writes and
-- house_doors() and ward_houses() count for the map's colours.
-- Run with tests/sql/run.sh; each test rolls back.

\ir fixtures.sql

-- test: map areas, and Kalonzo's Nairobi wards

  assert (select map_area from public.campaigns where slug = 'sakaja') = 'nairobi';

  assert (select map_area from public.campaigns where slug = 'mathira') = 'nyeri/mathira';
  assert (select count(*) from public.wards
           where campaign_id = 'ca000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001') = 85, 'Kalonzo has 85 wards';

    'the 2022 register, 2,415,310';
  assert (select count(*) from public.wards

end $$;
```
